# Foundation Backlog

These are the smallest engineering tasks needed for reproducible development, relevant checks and reliable verification, in priority order. Each one traces to findings in [LIQ-003](LIQ-003-engineering-baseline.md). Implementing all 172 capabilities is not a prerequisite for anything here; most stay "not assessed" until real work activates them.

This list is a planning record. Once decision D6 picks a tracker, each item becomes a work item there and this file links to it instead of describing it ([ENG-002 §2](framework/ENG-002-documentation-system.md#2-what-belongs-where)). Nothing here is authorized for implementation until the owner says so. FB-02 was authorized on 2026-10-21, and FB-19 and FB-20 the same day ([LIQ-001 §1](LIQ-001-project-profile.md#recorded-instructions-that-shape-engineering-work)). On 2026-11-04 the owner asked for the approved Spree baseline to be completed with this backlog, the completion audit and the recorded decisions as the plan. The owner delegated the routine technical, image and fidelity acceptance of approved baseline work, and its merging; gap answers and owner decisions stay with the owner ([record](verification/2026-11-04-delegated-acceptance-and-merging.md)).

## Open isolation findings

Reproduced on 2026-10-20 at backend `394b53f` with real requests and nothing stubbed (LIQ-003, evidence C3). They stay open until the option A validation (FB-03) and the fixes that follow it pass on PostgreSQL with same-store controls.

| # | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|
| ISO-1 | A store's admin sees shoppers of other stores in the customer list | Open |
| ISO-2 | A store's admin can open another store's shopper record | Open |
| ISO-3 | A store's admin can change another store's shopper record | Open |
| ISO-4 | A shopper of one store can sign in at another store | Open |
| ISO-5 | Registering at one store fails if the email has an account at another store, which also reveals that the account exists | Open |
| ISO-6 | One store's tax rate marks another store's tax setup task as done | Open |
| ISO-7 | Customer order totals appear to be summed across stores | Seen in code, not tested |

What this evidence does not show yet: same-store controls, a before-and-after check on the tax task, any PostgreSQL run, and the wider surfaces listed in the merchant lifecycle specification. The reproduction itself stays out of this public repository while the defect is open.

**FB-03's validation, 2026-11-04.** The option A validation ran on 2026-11-04 in the cloud environment, at `main` `87a2ea8`, on PostgreSQL and SQLite, with same-store controls, as the owner's answers to N1 to N6 set out. It finished the same day. Its report, tests and logs are kept in the private project notes and are not published while the findings are open; any further finding it reports is the owner's to accept, and is described only there. Its result waits for the owner's acceptance (N1), and so does the choice it prepared: whether a shopper account belongs to a store or to a business. Until the owner accepts the result, the findings above keep their statuses. The validation fixed nothing. On 2026-11-09 the owner gave decisions on it in the conversation ([record](verification/2026-11-09-owner-decision-brief-answers-4.md), item 6). **Status corrected on 2026-11-11 (owner's instruction, [FB-23](work-items/fb-23-remediation-ledger.md) §3):** the private validation already ran and found isolation failures. Remediation is paused by the reported platform block, and current `main` has not been revalidated since that run. When the block is legitimately resolved, the authorized private remediation and its verification are completed. The non-Figma engineering milestone is not declared complete while isolation is broken or unverified. No fix has started, ISO-1 to ISO-7 stay open, and a multi-tenant release stays blocked. The private evidence is kept as it is. Which findings are accepted, and who owns a shopper account, stay pending.


## Existing defects found by the baseline

**Since 2026-11-11 these defects are being fixed.** The owner's instruction of 2026-11-11 replaced the earlier instruction to defer fixes; the paragraph below keeps the history. [FB-23](work-items/fb-23-remediation-ledger.md) is the completion ledger: each open defect's owner, implementation, evidence and closure condition, the fix order, and the reconciled totals (91 open: 14 High, 36 Medium and 41 Low; SD-8 and SD-21 closed; SD-78 an observation; 94 rows in all). The "92 open" reported on 2026-11-11 was a miscount.

Spree or storefront behaviour at the pinned revisions that stops a shopper or staff member, loses a record the business needs, or misinforms the developers who build on Spree's APIs. Tracked here at the owner's instruction of 2026-10-22. The baseline reproduces each one and changes nothing: the baseline pull requests keep the behaviour as it is, and their stories and captures show it. Whether and how to fix one is decided after the baseline, with the rest of Liquorify's own work ([LIQ-001 §1](LIQ-001-project-profile.md#recorded-instructions-that-shape-engineering-work)), unless the owner brings it forward. Reporting one to Spree is also the owner's call; nothing has been reported. On 2026-11-04 the owner decided that SD-8 and SD-11 are not reported to Spree, and that the same instruction stands for every other defect here: none is reported to Spree or to a library's maintainers ([decisions 21 and 37](verification/2026-11-04-owner-decision-brief-answers.md)). Other differences the batches found stay in their records as behaviour, not defects.

| Severity | Meaning |
|---|---|
| High | A shopper or staff member cannot complete a task, or a record the business needs, such as a consent, is missing or wrong |
| Medium | The task completes, but the screen, or for a developer the kit, shows wrong or misleading information |
| Low | Layout, wording or styling only, including a published example that is out of date |

Risk classes follow [LIQ-001 §4](LIQ-001-project-profile.md#4-review-and-verification-expectations) and depend on effects, including for documentation. "Repo" is B for `liquorify` and S for `liquorify-storefront`. Each item is gated by its own actual dependencies; unrelated decisions do not block bounded work.

Packages passed on `main` afterwards, and the audit run by hand on `main` passed, found nothing new to fix and opened no pull request; it still reports the `deepmerge` advisory as unresolved, with no ignore entry ([record](verification/2026-11-04-fb-11-security-audit-repair.md)). ### Owner action steps

**OA-4: superseded.** Nothing to do. The steps for a Chromatic account were listed here before Q10 replaced Chromatic with Playwright on 2026-10-21. If a Chromatic project or a `CHROMATIC_PROJECT_TOKEN` secret was created in the meantime, nothing uses it, and it can be deleted.

**OA-5: Figma.** The billing choice, shown before any purchase as Q4 asks:

- **Do not use the existing Figma team.** Its plan is one Figma restricts to non-commercial use, so its seat is not suitable for Liquorify's work (read through the Figma connection on 2026-10-21).
- **The choice:** a new team on the **Professional** plan with **one Full seat**, for you. No Dev or Collab seats, and not the Organization plan.
- **The price**, from Figma's published euro prices on 2026-10-21, before tax: **€20 a month billed monthly**, or **€16 a month billed annually** (€192 for the year). Figma's US page shows $16 a month billed annually. The price at checkout is the one that counts; if it differs, stop and say so.
- **Recommended: billed monthly.** It costs €4 a month more, but it can be stopped any month while the reproduction proves itself, and it can be switched to annual later.

The steps, once you have decided:

1. Sign in at figma.com with the account you will use for Liquorify's design work.
2. Open the team menu at the top of the sidebar and choose **Create new team**. Name it `Liquorify`.
3. Choose the **Professional** plan, the billing period you picked, and one Full seat.
4. Pay, and check that the receipt shows the plan, the period and one Full seat.
5. Confirm the purchase, with the team's name. A session then helps you connect Figma and checks three things: that the connection uses the account you intend for Liquorify; that it reaches the `Liquorify` team, on the Professional plan, with the Full seat able to edit; and that the connector can create and edit a design in that team, shown by creating one small check file there and editing it, not by reading alone. OA-5 is marked done only when all three are verified. The check file is kept or removed as you choose.

## Recommended next work item

**FB-02: run the backend's existing checks on `liquorify` and protect `main` in both repositories.**

**Status (2026-10-21).** In progress; the open parts need the owner. [Pull request #2](https://github.com/pateldhruval872/liquorify/pull/2) merged at 02:36 UTC. The first check attempt was BLOCKED because Actions was off ([record](verification/2026-10-21-fb-02-pull-request-checks.md)). Once the owner turned it on (OA-2), all 29 pull request jobs passed at `0e86f0b`; Codecov refused the coverage uploads, which does not fail a job ([record](verification/2026-10-21-fb-02-first-workflow-run.md)). Both workflows passed again on the merge push, and the owner saved the branch rulesets in both repositories (OA-3; [record](verification/2026-10-21-fb-02-merge-push-and-protection.md)). Against the acceptance criteria below:

1. Met.
2. Nothing to record, since no job failed.
3. Met for `packages.yml`: its six release jobs skipped on the merge push. The `release.yml` and `docs-package.yml` guards are shown only by reading until their first trigger.
4. Met for required checks, by GitHub's reported merge state. The refusal of a direct push is unverified, because the owner asked that it not be tested by pushing to `main`.
5. Holds.

Whether FB-02 closes with those two parts open is the owner's call.

Why this one: the backend has a large test suite, but it had never run on this repository before FB-02, and nothing stopped an unverified change from reaching `main` in either repository. Every later result, including the option A validation, is only as reliable as the checks behind it. It changes no application code. FB-03 can proceed in parallel in the cloud once its own decisions are made.

- **Outcome.** The backend's existing workflows have one recorded run at a known revision, upstream publishing jobs cannot run here, and both `main` branches accept changes only through pull requests with required checks.
- **In scope.** The workflow changes in the table below; one run of `tests.yml` and `packages.yml` on the FB-02 pull request; branch protection in both repositories once those checks have reported.
- **Out of scope.** Fixing failing upstream tests, changing dependencies, adding Liquorify tests, adding credentials, any deployment.

| Repository | Workflow file | Change in FB-02 | Proposed required checks (source: GitHub Actions) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `liquorify` | `.github/workflows/tests.yml` | Add a manual trigger; drop the pull request path filter | Reported and passing at `0e86f0b` ([record](verification/2026-10-21-fb-02-first-workflow-run.md)): `Build (PostgreSQL)`, `PostgreSQL - core (1/3)`, `PostgreSQL - core (2/3)`, `PostgreSQL - core (3/3)`, `PostgreSQL - api (1/1)`, `PostgreSQL - emails (1/1)`, `PostgreSQL - providers (1/1)`, `PostgreSQL - dashboard (1/1)`, `PostgreSQL - mcp (1/1)`, `PostgreSQL - opentelemetry (1/1)`, `Brakeman`. Advisory until Liquorify decides on MySQL: `Build (MySQL)` and the nine `MySQL - …` jobs |
| `liquorify` | `.github/workflows/packages.yml` | Add a manual trigger; drop the pull request path filter; limit its six `… - Release` jobs to the upstream repository | Reported and passing at `0e86f0b`: `Typecheck, Test & Build`, `Scaffolded app - Typecheck & Lint`, `SDK Integration Tests`, `Dashboard - E2E` (the job that summarizes the four `Dashboard - E2E (n/4)` shards) |
| `liquorify` | `.github/workflows/release.yml` | Limit `Release gems to RubyGems` to the upstream repository | None |
| `liquorify` | `.github/workflows/docs-package.yml` | Limit its `Release` job to the upstream repository | None |
| `liquorify` | `.github/workflows/security-audit.yml` | None; it already has a manual trigger | None: it runs on a schedule, not on pull requests |
| `liquorify-storefront` | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | None | `Lint`, `Type Check`, `Unit Tests`, all reported and passing on `main` at `ac53c5b`. `E2E (Playwright + Spree)` stays advisory. OA-1 is done and a run passed on 2026-10-23, on storefront #18; making it required is a protection change for the owner |

`Build (PostgreSQL)` is required as well as the test jobs because a job skipped after its build fails reports as skipped, and GitHub accepts a skipped check as satisfying a required check.

**What stands in the way of running these checks from a pull request branch:**

1. **Actions was off** (OA-2, done). No workflow started on the FB-02 pull request until the owner turned Actions on, on 2026-10-21. The next commit then started both workflows.
2. **Path filters.** On `main`, `tests.yml` runs on a pull request only when it changes `spree/**` or the workflow file, and `packages.yml` only for its listed paths, so a documentation-only pull request, such as the one that added this folder, runs neither. FB-02 drops both pull request filters, so both run on every pull request, including its own. The push filters stay.
3. **Required checks and path filters conflict.** A required check from a path-filtered workflow never reports on a pull request outside those paths, so that pull request waits indefinitely. The other option was one always-running summary job per workflow, with only those required. The owner chose to drop the filters for now (2026-10-21); runner minutes are free while the repository is public.
4. **The manual trigger works only after merge.** GitHub offers a manual trigger only for a workflow whose file on the default branch declares it, so until FB-02 merges, the pull request run is the evidence.
5. **Checks must report before they can be required.** GitHub's settings offer a check for selection once it has reported in the repository, and names must match exactly; a matrix change renames its checks.
6. **Release guards must ship in the same change.** Merging FB-02 is a push to `main` that changes `packages.yml`, which would start its release jobs. Release jobs never run on a pull request branch, so their guards are verified by reading the files and, after merge, by the jobs' skipped results.
7. **Storefront branch protection** looked as if it needed a paid GitHub plan, because the repository was private. It became public on 2026-10-21 and the owner kept it public, so GitHub Free enforces its rules ([record](verification/2026-10-21-fb-02-merge-push-and-protection.md#enforcement)).

One consequence of requiring checks, rather than a blocker to running them: the weekly dependency audit opens its pull requests with the default token, which does not start CI, so once checks are required those pull requests wait until CI is started another way ([LIQ-005](LIQ-005-spree-engineering-adoption.md) SA-28).

- **Acceptance criteria.**
  1. `tests.yml` and `packages.yml` each have a run on a recorded `liquorify` revision. A verification record in `liquorify/` lists each job as PASS, FAIL, BLOCKED or NOT_RUN, with a link to the run.
  2. Every failing job is recorded as FAIL with an owner and a follow-up item. Nothing is skipped or marked as expected to fail.
  3. Every publishing job carries a condition that limits it to the upstream repository, confirmed by reading the workflow files. The six `packages.yml` release jobs show as skipped on the merge push; the `release.yml` and `docs-package.yml` jobs show as skipped on the first tag or documentation change after it.
  4. `main` in both repositories refuses direct pushes and requires the agreed checks before merge, shown on one pull request in each.
  5. No application code, dependency, credential or database changes.
- **Risk.** Protected: it changes CI configuration and repository settings. Its verification is the workflow runs and the branch protection demonstration above, not application or isolation tests.
- **Decisions.**
  - Made on 2026-10-21: the CI file edits are authorized, the pull request path filters are dropped, and MySQL checks stay advisory until Liquorify decides whether it supports MySQL.
  - Made at the final approval step on 2026-10-21: the owner merged pull request #2 and saved the branch protection settings and required checks below.
  - Made on 2026-10-21: protection is not tested by pushing directly to `main`, and the storefront stays public.
  - Decision D7 (backend visibility) does not block this item. The suite is free to run only while the repository is public.
- **Limitations.** A green upstream suite shows only that Spree's own tests pass. It says nothing about tenant isolation, which stays open under ISO-1 to ISO-7. If the storefront's plan does not include branch protection, that is recorded as a limitation rather than worked around.

### Proposed branch protection settings

Applied by the owner on 2026-10-21 as one "Protect main" ruleset per repository, matching this table ([record](verification/2026-10-21-fb-02-merge-push-and-protection.md#saved-rulesets)). Each repository gets one ruleset for its default branch (Settings, then Rules, then Rulesets), or a classic branch protection rule with the same effect.

| Setting | `liquorify` and `liquorify-storefront` | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Target | The default branch, `main` | — |
| Require a pull request before merging | On, with 0 required approvals | GitHub does not let authors approve their own pull requests, and one person has access, so a required approval would block every merge (LIQ-001 §2). Owner review stays a process rule; raise it to 1 when a second reviewer joins |
| Require status checks to pass | On, with the proposed required checks in the table above, each from the GitHub Actions source | Checks from the workflow files, not from another app reporting the same name |
| Require branches to be up to date before merging | On | The checks then cover what `main` becomes after the merge, which LIQ-001 §4 asks for. With one person merging, the extra runs are rare |
| Require conversation resolution before merging | On | Review comments are answered before merge |
| Block force pushes, restrict deletions | On | Keeps `main`'s history |
| Bypass list | Empty, so the rule applies to administrators too | Acceptance criterion 4 asks `main` to refuse direct pushes. Turning the rule off for an urgent change is itself a Protected change |

Effects to expect:

- **Backend checks are selectable now.** GitHub offers a check once it has reported in the repository. Every name in the backend list reported on 2026-10-21, exactly as written above ([record](verification/2026-10-21-fb-02-first-workflow-run.md)).
- **Every backend pull request runs the full suite,** documentation included, because the path filters are gone. Runner minutes are free while the repository is public (D7).
- **Dependency audit pull requests wait.** The weekly audit opens them with the default token, which does not start CI, so their required checks do not report until CI is started another way ([LIQ-005](LIQ-005-spree-engineering-adoption.md) SA-28).
- **A renamed job needs the list updated** in the same change, or pull requests wait for a check that no longer exists.
- **The storefront is public** since 2026-10-21, so GitHub Free enforces its rules. If it were made private again, they would need GitHub Pro or a team plan.

## Spree baseline reproduction work items

These reproduce and document what Spree already has, so that Figma, Storybook and Playwright's visual checks start from Spree's real screens, components and tokens, and later Liquorify work extends them rather than redrawing them. **Spree baselining activates them**: each starts once FB-18 is complete and reviewed and the owner instructs it. None waits for a Liquorify component, Liquorify branding or the acceptance of LIQ-007 as a whole. The tool choices they need were decided on 2026-10-21 (LIQ-007 Q4, Q7 and Q10, which superseded Q5), and [LIQ-010](LIQ-010-design-system-reproduction-architecture.md) plans how they are built, checked and kept up to date.

Rules for all three:

- **The code is the source.** Each reproduction copies Spree at the pinned revisions (backend `1d1f2d4`, storefront `ac53c5b`, or a later pair recorded in LIQ-008 and LIQ-009 first). A difference between a reproduction and the code is a defect in the reproduction.
- **Spree's look, names and states only.** No Liquorify branding, no new components and no redesigns.
- **Invented sample data only,** such as Spree's sample catalog. Never real merchant or shopper data.
- **Coverage is recorded against the IDs.** LIQ-008 and LIQ-009 gain a column showing, for each ID, where it is reproduced. A new ID added after an upstream update (FB-12) is reproduced in the same change that records it.
- **Each item closes with a verification record** in `liquorify/verification/`, listing what was built, what was compared, the results (PASS, FAIL, BLOCKED or NOT_RUN) and what could not be reproduced, with the reason.

- **Outcome.** Every existing component in LIQ-009 is documented in Storybook with its meaningful variants and states, and every story has a committed reference screenshot that Playwright compares on pull requests.
- **In scope.**
  - One Storybook in the backend workspace (React with Vite) for `@spree/dashboard-ui` (CMP-DUI-P, CMP-DUI-C), `@spree/dashboard-core` (CMP-CORE, its shared pages and product form modules), and the dashboard's and seller panel's components (CMP-DA-G, CMP-SP-G), with stand-in providers and mocked API responses.
  - One Storybook in the storefront (Next.js with Vite) for CMP-SF-P, CMP-SF-G and the page-level components, with mocked data. Server components are covered where Storybook's experimental support renders them; the rest are recorded.
  - Token pages showing every TK-DUI and TK-SF token with its light and dark values, read from the stylesheets rather than retyped, and the email branding defaults (TK-EM).
  - Playwright screenshot comparisons against each built Storybook ([LIQ-010 §11](LIQ-010-design-system-reproduction-architecture.md#11-visual-checks-with-playwright)): one pinned container image with Chromium, deterministic sample data, reference images committed beside the stories, and comparison reports and failure images published as CI artifacts with a set retention. An advisory workflow runs them on pull requests that change components, stories, styles or their configuration.
- **Out of scope.** Changing any component or token; Liquorify components; the accessibility target and any accessibility gate (FB-15); making the visual check a required check; accepting a changed screenshot automatically in CI; the email templates, which have their own previews.
- **Completion criteria.**
  1. Every component ID in LIQ-009 §2 to §5 has a story, or LIQ-009 names the story that shows it and says why it has none of its own (for example, a part only ever rendered inside its parent).
  2. Each story covers the component's meaningful variants and states, such as default, disabled, loading, empty, error, invalid, selected and open, in each theme its surface shows: light and dark for the dashboard and the seller panel, and light for the storefront, which never shows its dark values ([LIQ-010 §8](LIQ-010-design-system-reproduction-architecture.md#8-themes-and-widths-captured)). The states found are recorded per component, because FB-20 uses them as Figma component properties.
  3. Every token in LIQ-009 §6 to §8 appears on a token page with its values.
  4. Both Storybooks build in CI from a recorded revision.
  5. Every story has a committed reference screenshot for each theme and width it is captured in, made in the pinned environment and reviewed in the pull request that added it. The advisory workflow compares them on the pull requests described above, publishes its report and any failure images as artifacts, and never writes or accepts a reference image itself. It is not a required check.
  6. Only development dependencies, Storybook and Playwright configuration, stories, mocks, reference images and the CI step are added. No application code or production dependency changes.
- **Risk.** Protected: it adds dependencies and CI steps.
- **Depends on.** FB-18 complete and reviewed, the owner's instruction, and LIQ-007's Q7 and Q10, all done on 2026-10-21. No owner action is needed now that OA-4 is superseded.

### FB-20: the existing design systems in Figma

- **Status.** In progress since 2026-10-21, at the owner's instruction. Until the seat exists (OA-5), only the token preparation runs: token files generated from the stylesheets, with a check that they match, and the palette colours the components use outside the tokens ([LIQ-010 §9](LIQ-010-design-system-reproduction-architecture.md#9-token-preparation-and-the-css-to-figma-mapping)).
- **Outcome.** Figma libraries that match the dashboard design system (shared by the admin dashboard and the seller panel), the storefront's system and the email branding values, so FB-21 can rebuild every screen from them and later Liquorify designs start from Spree's real parts.
- **In scope.**
  - Variables for every TK-DUI, TK-SF and TK-EM token, with the code's names and light and dark as modes; text and effect styles from the theme entries (fonts, type scale, radius and shadows); and the Tailwind palette colours the components use, which LIQ-009 adds with the token preparation.
  - Figma components for every CMP-DUI-P, CMP-DUI-C and CMP-SF-P component, named as in the code, with the variants and states FB-19 recorded, using only the library's variables.
  - Figma components for the CMP-CORE, CMP-SF-G and application components that FB-21's screens reuse.
  - The Lucide icons the components use.
- **Out of scope.** Liquorify brand values (FB-14); new or changed components; linking Figma components to code (Code Connect) unless the owner chooses it (LIQ-007 Q4).
- **Completion criteria.**
  1. Every token in LIQ-009 §6 to §8 is a Figma variable or style with the same name, and its light and dark values match the stylesheets, checked against an export rather than by eye.
  2. Every CMP-DUI-P, CMP-DUI-C and CMP-SF-P component is a published Figma component with the variants and states FB-19 recorded for it.
  3. LIQ-009 records, against each component ID, whether it is in Figma; every component FB-21's screens use is.
  4. Each Figma component was compared with its Playwright reference screenshots in each theme, and each difference was fixed in Figma or recorded with a reason.
- **Risk.** Protected where it adds accounts or paid seats: light and dark modes and shared libraries need a paid Figma plan (OA-5).
- **Depends on.** FB-18 complete and reviewed, the owner's instruction, and LIQ-007's Q4, all done on 2026-10-21; OA-5 for everything inside Figma. Tokens start at once; each component follows its FB-19 stories, which record its states.

### FB-21: the existing screens, wireframes and flows in Figma

- **Outcome.** Every screen in LIQ-008, with its meaningful states, exists in Figma as a wireframe and as a full reproduction built from FB-20's libraries, and every flow FL-01 to FL-24 is a prototype across those screens.
- **In scope.**
  - A wireframe for every screen ID: layout, content order and navigation, without visual styling, grouped by surface and area as in LIQ-008.
  - A full reproduction of every screen ID at the dashboard's and seller panel's desktop width, and at mobile and desktop widths for the storefront, for each meaningful state LIQ-008 lists, including the shared list and detail states and the views of a role without write permission.
  - Every overlay (DA-OVL, SP-OVL, SF-OVL) and every email (EM-C, EM-O, EM-S, EM-SF) in its default branding.
  - A prototype for every flow FL-01 to FL-24, linking its screens and emails in order, naming the role that takes it.
  - Captures of a running instance as references, replaced by frames built from the libraries.
- **Out of scope.** Liquorify screens and changes; redesigns; the development previews SF-DEV-01 and SF-DEV-02, which only preview the storefront emails covered by EM-SF; layouts and redirects, which have no screens of their own.
- **Completion criteria.**
  1. Every screen ID in LIQ-008 §3 to §5, except SF-DEV, has a wireframe and a full reproduction, each named with its ID.
  2. Each meaningful state LIQ-008 names for a screen has a frame.
  3. Every overlay and email ID has a frame.
  4. Every flow FL-01 to FL-24 is a prototype from its first step to its last.
  5. Full reproductions use only FB-20's components and variables, with no detached copies or raw colour values, shown by a recorded check of the file.
  6. Each full reproduction was compared with the running screen at the pinned revisions, and each difference was fixed or recorded with a reason.
  7. LIQ-008 records, against each screen, overlay, email and flow ID, that it is in Figma.
- **Risk.** Standard, or Protected where it adds seats.
- **Depends on.** FB-20's libraries; a running instance of the dashboard, seller panel and storefront at the pinned revisions with sample data (FB-04, or the dashboard's browser-test setup); the owner's instruction.

Four words stay apart, at the owner's instruction of 2026-10-22:

- **Built** means the work exists on a branch.
- **Coverage** means what exists for an ID: a mapped capability, stories and their images, captured screens, rendered emails, Spree's own tests, or a named gap. Coverage never says that a flow works.
- **Functionally verified** means each flow or behaviour was shown to work end to end at a recorded revision: PASS, PARTIAL (some steps or behaviours passed and the rest are named), BLOCKED (it cannot run until something named exists), FAIL, or NOT_RUN.
- **Accepted** means the owner merged it and recorded the acceptance. A gap counts as deferred only once the owner has accepted it in the [gap register](#deferred-gaps-and-the-owners-acceptance).

### Deferred gaps and the owner's acceptance

Each gap a batch leaves open, with the batch's proposal for it. A gap stays open until evidence closes it or the owner explicitly accepts its deferral; the owner's answer is recorded here with its date. On 2026-10-22 the owner chose to answer pull request by pull request, as each is reviewed: #9, then #11, then storefront #18 with #12. G-01 was accepted on 2026-10-22, after #9's merge. G-18 was closed on 2026-10-23, when the owner accepted storefront #18's passing end-to-end run as its evidence. G-03, G-04, G-05, G-07 and G-15 were closed the same day; G-19, G-20 and G-21 were accepted, G-22 was deferred to a named follow-up, and G-23 was linked to G-11 (below). Every other gap is pending. After #11's merge the same day, the owner accepted part 1 and chose to leave its gaps, G-03 to G-10, pending. After the merges of #12 and storefront #18 on 2026-10-23, the owner accepted the shopper accounts batch and chose to leave its gaps, G-11 to G-17, pending. After the merges of #13 and storefront #19 the same day, the owner accepted the platform batch's step P1 and chose to leave the gaps it holds evidence for, G-03, G-04, G-05, G-15 and G-16, pending. Later that day the owner closed G-03, G-04, G-05 and G-15 against P1's reviewed mail and password reset evidence, and kept G-16 pending for the remaining email configuration and the webhook deliveries. Reviewing part 2 (#14) that evening, before its merge, the owner closed G-07, accepted G-19 as G-01 was accepted, accepted G-20 and G-21 as simulated interface-state coverage, deferred G-22 to the platform batch's step P6, and linked G-23 to G-11 ([record](verification/2026-10-22-fb-19-batch-staff-access-part-2.md#11-the-owners-review-2026-10-23)). Batch 2 (#16) proposed G-24 to G-32 on 2026-10-24 ([record §7](verification/2026-10-24-fb-19-batch-developer-settings.md#7-gaps)). When the owner accepted batch 2 on 2026-10-25, the owner kept all nine pending ([record §11](verification/2026-10-24-fb-19-batch-developer-settings.md#11-the-owners-review-2026-10-25)). When the owner accepted the platform batch's step P2 later that day, the owner closed G-29, G-30 and G-31, kept G-32 pending, and kept G-16 pending for the email configuration alone ([P2's record §9](verification/2026-10-25-fb-19-platform-p2.md#9-the-owners-answer-2026-10-25)). Batch 3 (#19) proposed G-33 to G-40 on 2026-10-26 ([record §7](verification/2026-10-26-fb-19-batch-setup-stores-and-settings.md#7-gaps)); G-36's deferral to batch 4 is the owner's answer of 2026-10-25 to the batch's second question, given before it was built. When the owner accepted batch 3 on 2026-10-26, the owner kept G-33 to G-35 and G-37 to G-40 pending and G-36 deferred, and asked that no gap be closed merely because the batch is accepted or merged ([record §11](verification/2026-10-26-fb-19-batch-setup-stores-and-settings.md#11-the-owners-review-2026-10-26)). Earlier that day the owner had said that G-40 belongs with platform step P7. The same day the owner brought G-11 forward to the platform batch's step [P7](work-items/fb-19-batch-platform.md#p7-refusals-within-one-store), which checks G-11, G-23, G-32 and G-40 now; each still closes only on passing evidence and the owner's acceptance. Part 1 of the shop batch (#21) proposed G-41 to G-45 on 2026-10-26 and recorded what it showed for G-02 ([record §7](verification/2026-10-26-fb-19-batch-storefront-shop-part-1.md#7-gaps)). The owner merged part 1 on 2026-10-27 and asked that the merge be recorded apart from acceptance, with part 1's acceptance and its unresolved gap decisions pending until the owner answers them explicitly ([merge record](verification/2026-10-27-fb-19-batch-storefront-shop-part-1-merge.md)); G-41 to G-45 stay pending. Delegated acceptance, from 2026-11-04, does not answer a gap. Accepting, deferring or linking one stays the owner's decision. A gap closes under delegated acceptance only on passing evidence for the closing condition its row records, and the row says it was closed that way ([record](verification/2026-11-04-delegated-acceptance-and-merging.md)). **Coverage** gaps are states or stories not shown on a running screen; **function** gaps are flow steps or behaviours not shown to work. Tenant isolation is an outstanding item above, not a gap here.

### Comparisons deferred to later batches

G-01, G-33, G-53, G-58, G-65, G-81 and, for the storefront, G-73 hold the stories a batch could not compare with a running screen. The owner accepted or deferred each so that a later batch whose running screen shows the state compares it. On 2026-11-04, deferring G-58 and G-65, the owner asked that each deferred check have a named work item and a closure condition (rows 11 and 19 of the owner decision brief, [record](verification/2026-11-04-owner-decision-brief-answers-2.md)). This is that work item, in each later dashboard batch: part 3 of the catalog and pricing batch, then batches 6, 8 and 10 to 13. On 2026-11-04 the owner deferred G-73 to the same work in the storefront's later batches, 12 and 13, whose checkouts use the same components, and to SD-21's fix, and G-81 to it as G-58 (rows 31 and 35 of the owner decision brief, [record](verification/2026-11-04-owner-decision-brief-answers-3.md)). Batch 6's G-85 is proposed to join it, as G-58, and so is batch 8's part 1's G-88. On 2026-11-09 the owner deferred batch 8's part 2's G-93 to it, with each of its 162 stories named, and assigned its 15 provider-specific stories to P4 (row 40 of the owner decision brief, [record §3](verification/2026-11-09-owner-decision-brief-answers-4.md#3-g-93s-remaining-stories)). On 2026-11-10 the owner deferred batch 10's G-96 to it, with each of its 39 stories given a destination in batch 12, batch 13 or this follow-up ([record §2](verification/2026-11-10-owner-answers-batch-10.md#2-g-96-each-storys-destination)).

- **What each batch does.** Its image review list has a section for earlier stories: each story held by one of these gaps that one of the batch's running screens shows, compared by the script as the batch's own stories are, with the gap it came from. A story compared there leaves its gap.
- **Closing.** A gap closes when every story it holds has been compared in one of those lists. When the last of those batches merges, a story still not compared keeps the status its gap gave it, seen in a capture or checked against its source, as G-01 set out, and its gap stays DEFERRED for those stories, each named.
- **Done so far.** Part 3 of the catalog and pricing batch (#61) found eight earlier stories its running screens show and compared them: three left G-33 and three left G-58, and the store date picker's two stayed in G-53, because its screens draw the picker in the other mode ([record §5.2](verification/2026-11-04-fb-19-batch-catalog-and-pricing-part-3.md#52-earlier-stories)). Batch 6 (#64) found fifteen and compared them: four left G-01, four G-33, three G-53 and four G-58 ([image review list](verification/2026-11-04-fb-19-batch-inventory-image-review.md#earlier-stories)). Batch 8's part 1 (#80) found six and compared them: two left G-33, three G-53 and one G-58 ([image review list](verification/2026-11-05-fb-19-batch-orders-returns-and-fulfilment-part-1-image-review.md#earlier-stories)). Its part 2 (#84) found two of part 1's own and compared them: both left G-88 ([image review list](verification/2026-11-05-fb-19-batch-orders-returns-and-fulfilment-part-2-image-review.md#earlier-stories)). Batch 11 (#89) found one and compared it: the channel select's "All channels" story, on the home page's filter, left G-88 ([image review list](verification/2026-11-10-fb-19-batch-home-reports-and-emails-image-review.md#earlier-stories)). Batch 13's part 1 (#98) found two and compared them: batch 7's delivery method list with a seller's methods left G-65, and the table toolbar's open filter panel left G-24; the seller panel's earlier stories wait for part 2 ([image review list](verification/2026-11-10-fb-19-batch-marketplace-part-1-image-review.md#earlier-stories)). Batch 10 (#94) found six and compared them: one left G-81, one G-33, two G-53 and two G-58 ([image review list](verification/2026-11-09-fb-19-batch-customers-image-review.md#earlier-stories)). Batch 13's part 2 (#101) found three and compared them: batch 7's package types page, empty and with its add sheet, left G-65, and batch 8 part 2's fulfil items form left G-93. The storefront stories a seller's checkout shows stay with G-73, because they are in the storefront's own Storybook ([image review list](verification/2026-11-10-fb-19-batch-marketplace-part-2-image-review.md#earlier-stories)). Batch 13 leaves its own stories not compared in G-111 and G-116. Batch 12's part 1 (#109) found twenty of G-96's and compared them: fourteen of the fifteen G-96 sent to it, on the company page, and six of its follow-up stories, on a wholesale buyer's customer page, all leaving G-96; S19 and eight follow-up stories keep their status with the comparison follow-up ([image review list](verification/2026-11-10-fb-19-batch-wholesale-image-review.md#earlier-stories)). It leaves its own one story not compared in G-125.
- **What it does not change.** A story seen in a capture or checked against its source is coverage, not a comparison, and no comparison is functional verification. The Stripe and EasyPost stories stay with G-67 and P4, batch 9's mocked provider stories with G-76: Stripe's with P4, and Adyen's and PayPal's accepted as source-reviewed coverage, and the stories of a digital file source with settings stay with G-55.

### Proposed order of the remaining batches

A proposal: the owner may reorder it. The dashboard and storefront tracks run side by side. Each batch starts once the batch it depends on is accepted.

|---|---|---|
| 1 | Dashboard shell part 2 (CAP-04-05; DA-OVL-01 to DA-OVL-04) | The owner's acceptance of part 1, given on 2026-10-22. Built in #14, accepted and merged on 2026-10-23 |

Proposed in #15 ([work item](work-items/fb-19-batch-developer-and-integration-settings.md)); the owner answered its questions on 2026-10-23: one pull request, P2 separately after its acceptance, and the listing framework's stories included. | 4 | Storefront shop, in two parts, approved by the owner on 2026-10-26 as lane A ([below](#lanes-beside-batch-3-agreed-on-2026-10-26)). Part 1: the home page, header, footer and menus, search, region and language, the policy page, and the error and not-found pages (CAP-05-17; SF-SHOP-01, SF-SHOP-06, SF-OVL-01, SF-OVL-03, SF-OVL-04, SF-SYS). **Who takes a freed slot.** FB-03's validation of tenant isolation took the first free slot, ahead of queued work (decision 18), and finished on 2026-11-04. SD-8's fix took the slot after it, in its own bounded pull request (decision 19). Multi-store administration took the slot SD-8's fix freed, on 2026-11-04, and was built and merged the same day in #49 ([merge record](verification/2026-11-04-fb-19-batch-multi-store-administration-merge.md)), which frees that slot again. The lanes' other queued work follows when a slot is available. The rules of 2026-10-26 below still apply to every lane.

**Running the lanes under the owner's delegation, from 2026-11-04.** The coordinating session opens a thread for each eligible work item and keeps at most three implementation lanes active. When a lane frees, the next eligible item in dependency order starts. Each thread implements its approved scope, runs its tests, reviews its images and fidelity, and opens a pull request with its evidence. The coordinating session gives the final review. It merges once the approved acceptance criteria and every required check pass on the final commit, records it as delegated acceptance, brings the other open pull requests up to date, and verifies the relevant checks on `main`. Questions for the owner are gathered in one brief, and work they do not block carries on ([record](verification/2026-11-04-delegated-acceptance-and-merging.md)).

### Lanes beside batch 3, agreed on 2026-10-26

At the owner's instruction of 2026-10-26, two lanes run beside batch 3 (#19), under the existing Spree-first baseline rules. Each runs in its own session, on its own branches and with its own test data. The owner chose them from a proposal that weighed every remaining batch: these two were the only baseline work whose prerequisites were all accepted.

| Lane | Work | Starts now | After the owner's acceptance of batch 3 and #19's merge, both given on 2026-10-26 |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | The storefront shop (batch 4), in two parts, each with a storefront pull request and a backend records pull request, as the accounts batch had. Part 1 comes first, starting with its work item and the owner's answers | Part 1. Part 2's stories, captures and comparison | Part 2's storefront checks for G-38 and its first-run setup test with sample data (G-36) |
| B | The platform batch's step [P7](work-items/fb-19-batch-platform.md#p7-refusals-within-one-store), refusals within one store, in its own pull request | G-11, G-23 and G-32 | G-40, which P7 now includes |

The rules both lanes follow:

1. **Separate branches and test data.** Each lane starts from `main` in its own checkout, and Spree's browser-test server keeps its database inside that checkout, so no lane's data reaches another's. Lane A's sample catalog stays in its own copy. If two lanes ever share a machine, each needs its own worktree and its own ports.
2. **Merge order.** #19 merges first. Then each lane's pull requests merge in the order the owner accepts them, with lane A's part 1 before part 2, and each part's storefront pull request together with its records pull request. The pull request that records this plan also merges after #19.
3. **After each merge.** Every other open lane pull request is brought up to date from `main`, and the checks that the merge could affect run again before it merges: the backend's required checks always, and a lane's visual, capture or refusal checks when the merge changed the shared test helpers in `packages/liquorify-storybook/fidelity/`, the stories they read, or what they check.
4. **Results from the evidence.** Each check reports PASS, PARTIAL or FAIL from what it showed. A gap closes only on passing evidence and the owner's acceptance; neither lane promises to close one.
5. **Tenant isolation stays unverified.** Both lanes keep every request and capture within one store and record tenant isolation as NOT_RUN, under FB-03 and ISO-1 to ISO-7.

**What waits.** The owner accepted batch 3 at `c3ffc4d` and #19 merged on 2026-10-26, so its dependent checks in both lanes can now run. Batches 5 and 7, the later dashboard batches and G-37's browser-test step depended on that acceptance too; each starts on the owner's instruction. On 2026-10-30 the owner asked for batch 5's proposal, which is in #33 ([work item](work-items/fb-19-batch-catalog-and-pricing.md)); the owner answered its questions on 2026-11-02, and part 1 was built in #34, accepted the same day before its merge, and merged on 2026-11-03. Part 2's detailed proposal is in #36, and the owner answered its questions on 2026-11-04, so its work can start. Part 3's proposal was made on 2026-11-04 in #43 under the owner's delegation, and merged the same day, with its choices recorded as decided under it, and its work starts after part 2's merge. Batch 9's proposal was made on 2026-11-04 in #45, with one question for the owner, which the owner answered the same day: the real Stripe states and G-13 go to P4. Batch 7 merged the same day in #41, and batch 9's work started. Figma waits for OA-5, still open on 2026-11-04. The private gift card fix merged in #30 on 2026-11-02, apart from the lanes; its open limitations stay in the private project notes (decision 36 of the owner decision brief). Liquorify-specific architecture and features stay deferred until the baseline is complete.
